#3052. Microsoft’s new Licensed Weights lets businesses customize AI models with their own data

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Microsoft is bridging the gap between proprietary and open-weight AI while targeting data-conscious businesses.

During the keynote at its Ignite conference on Tuesday, Microsoft CEO Satya Nadella announced several new models from Microsoft AI (MAI), its lab within the company. But new model releases are almost a dime a dozen these days, and accelerating fast — what really stood out was how Microsoft will let its customers access those models.

Also: Microsoft says its new AI coding model rivals Claude Opus 5.5

MAI said it would allow its customers to customize its frontier models with their own data and run them securely in Azure through an initiative called Licensed Weights. It’s essentially a selective approach to open-weight AI: more so than proprietary models from OpenAI and Anthropic, but not fully downloadable on Hugging Face like Mistral’s Le Chonk. Interested companies will need to go through their Microsoft account reps to get started.

“You control who can access it. Your data and adaptations stay entirely yours. And we’ll work with you through Frontier Company to deliver the custom model and embed it in your own products and agents,” Mustafa Suleyman, CEO of MAI, said during the keynote.

The approach should stand out to businesses looking for affordable AI that lets them retain control over their IP. Microsoft said it would only use customers’ information to train its models if they agreed.

“No one wants to pour their IQ into someone else’s model,” Suleyman said during the keynote, a nod to an argument Palantir CEO Alex Karp has made repeatedly.

The dominant, if simplified, narrative in AI has been that proprietary, expensive models boast peak capabilities but aren’t customizable, while open, cheaper models are customizable but less capable. Microsoft’s AI models don’t necessarily compete with OpenAI and Anthropic yet, but the company has legacy sway with countless businesses. By offering a customizable AI option, it could strike a balance among major tech companies.

Western labs, including Thinking Machines and Mistral, are releasing more competitive open-weight models, but Anthropic and OpenAI still have the overwhelming majority of the enterprise AI market. Though Licensed Weights isn’t an open-weight family of models, it does diversify the open-versus-closed debate a bit and differentiate Microsoft’s offering from OpenAI’s and Anthropic’s. It’s also a smart pairing with Microsoft’s goal to balance price and performance, if businesses find it impressive enough.

Also: Microsoft unveiled Copilot Workspaces at Ignite 2026

“Averaged over four widely used benchmarks that target the everyday enterprise coding tasks we see in GitHub Copilot, MAI Code 2 gives you 98% of the performance of GPT 6.1 Sol at just 40% of the cost, and against Opus 5.5 it’s an incredible 5% of that cost,” Suleyman said of the new coding model the company announced at Ignite.

The company also released its new image editing model, MAI Image 2.7, on Tuesday, which now leads the category on Artificial Analysis while beating Nano Banana Pro and several GPT image models on price and quality. With Licensed Weights, Microsoft has positioned itself for organizations seeking a reliable blend of ROI tools, customization, security controls, and capability.

#3051. The hidden iOS 27 hack I use to turn my iPhone Duo into a distraction-free Kindle killer — here's how to do it

An exclusive iPhone Duo shortcut for book fans

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The iPhone Duo can do a lot of things: multitasking, note-taking and gaming to name a few. But I've been using it to catch up on my reading list, because the 7.6-inch inner display is perfectly proportioned to replace that new Kindle you may have been eyeing up in Black Friday sales.

The best Kindles, and other ereaders, have always positioned themselves as a distraction-free reading experience: You use them for reading books (or comics) and nothing else. There's some truth to that; I would often pick up the iPhone Duo intending to read a chapter or two during my commute only to be led astray by the siren call of my Duolingo streak or a WhatsApp message that needed replying to.

Not anymore! Thanks to a neat update to Apple's Shortcuts app that's only available on the iPhone Duo, you can automate a shortcut based on the Duo's folded state. When you pair this with Apple Books (or another reading app), you can stop interruptions or distractions before they happen.

Open the Shortcuts app on your iPhone Duo, and tap the + button in the bottom corner, then tap Edit in the top right corner.

Tap on "Automation" beneath the text box and scroll down until you see the option for "Open or Closed" underneath the "Display" heading. Tap it to add the instruction to your Shortcut.

Navigate back to your Shortcut menu and tap on "Open App". The command will appear on the left-hand pane. Once added, tap the "App" on the left and select Apple Books from the drop-down menu that appears.

Selecting Apple Books will, by default, open the app to your current book. If you want to specify a different command — like seeing opening directly into a curated list of the books you want to read, you can do that by tapping the Apple Books app itself on the right pane and adding the instruction to your list of commands.

Once you've set up this Shortcut, the iPhone Duo will launch directly into your current book when you unfold it. If you want to get back to your home screen, all you need to do is swipe up — but this is a handy little tip for always putting your reading habit first.

#3050. Sigma (SGML) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

Debt refinancing and a return to full operations shaped the quarter.

By Motley Fool Transcribing – Nov 18, 2026 at 6:23PM EST

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Sigma Lithium (SGML -5.38%) reported financial results for the third quarter of 2026, highlighted by a $125 million debt refinancing and the resolution of environmental regulatory challenges in Brazil. Management reported that the issuance of five-year convertible bonds enabled the full repayment of secured debt, significantly extending the company's debt maturity profile and lowering short-term liabilities. Despite temporary operational suspensions during the quarter that impacted sales volumes and revenue, the company maintained unit production costs below current market prices and resumed full operations in early October. The company stated it is now focused on its multiyear growth strategy, which includes expanding industrial capacity to 830,000 tons per year by 2028.

Ana Cabral Gardner: So I'll answer questions one by one. First, it was regarding the costs. They were neither capitalized nor excluded. Operator: We have one more question from Ailing Chen from Hotwind. Sigma Lithium has been listed on the ASX for more than 2 months, but there appears to be virtually no trading activity or meaningful share price quotation. What is your view on this situation? Ana Cabral Gardner: Absolutely. And the convertible bond note that I was just referring to in the previous answer is the answer to the question. So the convertible bond is converted into CDIs, which are the ASX listed shares. Again, the dilution was approximately 14%.

So there's going to be a number of shares that when and if the bond is converted, will flow into the ASX market, hopefully beginning the trading activity you referred to. We're doing our part to act on that listing in order to foster the liquidity and the trading and the note was a demonstration of it. Our first instrument issued was already in Australia.

Operator: The next question comes from Katie Lachapelle from Canaccord Genuity. I want to clarify your comments regarding debt. Is the plan to repay the Synergy debt before year-end instead of in 2027, but then also bring the additional debt?

Ana Cabral Gardner: Katie, well, thanks for the question. Good to see you. Yes, that's exactly the plan. We decided to do the conversion because the refinancing of the debt saves us quite a lot of cash. With the debt reset and the interest rate increases of SOFR, that debt would end up almost in 14% following the reset in December. So our decision is to pay the debt immediately -- immediately after the issuance of the convertible bond closing. We'll pay the debt immediately after the convertible bond closes, which is imminent. So essentially, Synergy will be completely repaid and removed from our balance sheet. And therefore, the balance sheet becomes unencumbered because Synergy is a secured creditor.

The plan going forward, as we've mentioned a few times, is to releverage the balance sheet for the growth strategy. And that will be done with what we call adequate type financing. In other words, as we bring in more debt for growth, we plan to bring in long-term low-cost debt along the same lines of the BNDES debt that we have. So most likely, once we begin this new stage of growth in 2027, we will bring -- we will unlock that credit line in order to tap from it to deliver the growth.

Operator: We have one more question for Katie. Hi Ana, can you provide more specific time lines around the planned growth at Phase 2? When will we open the mine? When you plan to construction commence? Or procurement for the Phase 2 plan components?

Ana Cabral Gardner: Yes. We plan to start opening Mine 2 shortly, very shortly. Most likely, it will be first quarter of next year or later this quarter. Now that will be the foundation of planning for the second plant. We have initiated the construction of the second plant in December 2024. We used quite a lot of our liquidity on it. We continued throughout the first quarter of '25. So what's remaining is procuring for equipment. We've had a procurement sheet, and we estimated based on that procurement sheet updated that there's approximately USD 80 million left of equipment to be ordered. We do not plan to do it now.

We do plan to do it most likely early in the year of 2027. There's a transition of governments here, both in the state and in some of the key positions in federal government. So once all of them are installed in the posts in their positions, we then will proceed on recommencing Mine 2 and Plant 2.

Operator: Next question comes from Rock Hoffman from JPMorgan. First, can you speak to the market dynamics around lithium tailings right now given lower lithium pricing dynamics? It is still a liquid market and would it be possible to sell the full 15,000 tons in the fourth quarter? Or will this material [indiscernible] need to be reprocessed to high grade to be sold?

Ana Cabral Gardner: Both things. It is possible to sell it. The lithium tailings market now looks a lot like it did almost a year ago when we made the decision to go and primarize the mine. Ex Works price for that material now hovers between $30 to $50 -- so that's a net profit. I would say, depending on the lithium quantity of the material, you would have a different Ex Works price. What we got on the plant probably will command a higher price of around $50-ish, perhaps even higher. We typically do not sell tailings against a falling lithium price as we've been seeing, observing in the last 2 weeks.

So we'll stay put and wait further into contract season to make a decision about the tailings. Even though ironically, this is when we get the most amount of offers because that product is very profitable for the client. Essentially, the client just transports it out and throws it into a flotation plant, converting it into very high-grade lithium concentrate. And an example is what's happening with a lot of the African content, which comes out in similar format and becomes very profitable on a fully integrated basis for the battery makers.

Some of these producers being able to live with numbers as low as the equivalent of $500 a ton, which for us, if you convert it back into the lithium content of the tailings benchmarks to approximately USD 30 to USD 50. So it triangulates well with lithium coming out of Nigeria, for example.

Operator: The second question from Rock Hoffman from JPMorgan is, how should we think about your offtake strategy after the main offtake is completed in a few months? Any discussions with partners for additional offtakes or prepayments agreement?

Ana Cabral Gardner: We do have a number of discussions with several partners because we're one of the few large producers with uncommitted material into 2027. We typically strike our offtakes on an annual basis. We strike contracts on an annual basis, and we are considering doing a multiyear offtake agreement into the 2027 year. But typically, for us, we've been achieving very successful commercial results by selling the material on maximum what we call a quarterly contract. For instance, the previous quarter of the third quarter, we achieved a $1,485 realized price, which was very robust if you were to take into account the falling lithium price environment. And we have been over the peers when it comes to achieved realized prices.

Why is that? As we're not hostages to an offtake, we don't price our products on formula. We use the market quote straight out of Shanghai Metals Market for concentrate, which essentially provides a more beneficial financial condition. But we are considering to do a multiyear offtake into 2027, but it's not something necessarily we have to do. We can perfectly survive continuing to do what we always do with selling material on a 1-year basis.

Operator: I'm returning to our CEO, Ana Cabral, for her final remarks.

Ana Cabral Gardner: Felipe and I and the entire Sigma team would like to thank you for your support throughout this quarter. We are very proud of how we came out of the third quarter into the fourth quarter. We came out stronger with tremendous visibility into our business, visibility such that the new governor of Minas Gerais has announced that he's going to take office from our region. So in January, he's actually going to become governor in our region in Vale do Jequitinhonha, which fills us with tremendous pride because we were able to shed the light in the transformational shared prosperity work we have been doing there for almost a decade.

And that has culminated in Sigma becoming this key company in the Western and Eastern global supply chains, delivering large scale, low cost and traceability. Going forward, that decision by the newly elected government has filled us with enthusiasm to full on continue on our growth strategy. And again, starting bottom up because the mine is what brings the most amount of jobs. And as a result, we plan to bring in another 25,000 people into the prosperity fold of Sigma. So working in an endeavor such as this is a partnership between the public powers, between society and between the company.

And as a good citizen company, we're really looking forward to contributing to lift the people of Vale do Jequitinhonha. And for that, I want to thank all of our stakeholders and our shareholders for being with us on this journey.

Operator: The third quarter of 2026 conference call of Sigma Lithium has concluded. For further information, please visit the company's website at www.sigmalithiumresources.com. You can disconnect now.

#3048. I’ve tested Ubuntu 27.04 ahead of its April release – and it will wow you

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Tech > Services & Software > Operating Systems > Linux

I've been using Ubuntu 27.04, and the speed boost is noticeable. Here's what else is coming.

Ubuntu 27.04, Tenacious T-Rex, is set for an April 22, 2027 release. Although that seems a long way off, it’s sooner than you think. As is implied by the release number, 27.04 is not an LTS release, so it only receives support for nine months (until January 2028). The good news is that you can migrate from 27.04 to 27.10, and then to the LTS release, 28.04. The entire upgrade path ensures that you don’t have to miss a single release.

But what’s coming in Ubuntu 27.04? Given that it’s an interim release, you’re probably assuming that there’ll be nothing exciting to be found. Although there won’t be any game-changing additions, there will be plenty of refinements and bug fixes.

Also: I’ve used Ubuntu for 22 years – and 26.10 might be its best release yet

I’ve been using the daily release of Ubuntu 27.04 for a bit. Let’s take a look at what is currently expected in Tenacious T-Rex.

This is probably the biggest addition to the next release of Ubuntu. And although you won’t find any major changes in GNOME, there are some important ones. First off, there will be editable quick settings, which allow for customizing, adding, removing, and rearranging toggles and widgets. GNOME 52 will also enjoy a revamped calendar popover, which streamlines the feature to focus primarily on events, time, and weather.

Also: Over Ubuntu? Fedora is the Linux distro I’d hop to next – here’s why

There will also be several improvements to window management, such as a spatial overview and a mosaic/tiling system for auto-resizing and organizing windows, a refined full-screen Alt+Tab app switcher, which will be capable of grouping older windows by application while showing newer ones individually, and finally, the ability to remember window sizing, placement, and workspace.

Desktop search is also getting a bit of polish by way of a redesigned popover and an improved results overlay that includes file previews, multiple actions, and search filters. And, as usual, there will be plenty of bug fixes and performance enhancements. The bump in speed is noticeable, too, in my testing.

Ubuntu 27.04 will ship with the Linux 7.6 kernel, which includes better VRAM management for gaming and expanded support for modern hardware, such as early enablement for AMD Zen 6, stable support for Intel Xe3P, mainline boot capabilities for Apple M3 Pro/Max/Ultra, and initial support for the 2026 Steam Controller.

There will also be performance gains for both the Btrfs and Ext4 file systems, which (along with the improvements in GNOME 52) will make Ubuntu 27.04 one of the best-performing releases in some time.

Also: This lightweight Linux distro revives old PCs destined for the junk pile – and it’s beautiful

One thing that surprised me is that Myna (Ubuntu’s AI dictation tool) has yet to find its way into the Ubuntu ISO as a default app. As with Ubuntu 26.10, you can install it, but you have to first add the necessary repository with:

With the repository added, you can install it with:

Once installed, you can use it as expected.

As of right now, that’s all we know about Tenacious T-Rex.

It’s so early in the release cycle that it still uses the default 26.10 wallpaper, but there will be more information soon. I will say this: I’ve come to count on Ubuntu’s latest releases to be an improvement over the previous iterations, and from what I’ve seen so far in my testing, 27.04 will not disappoint. It may not knock you back to prehistoric times, but Tenacious T-Rex will wow you with its performance and polished looks.

Also: Linux has hundreds of distros: 5 reasons to stop using the same few

It’s been a very long time since Canonical had a disappointing release, and 27.04 will keep up that trend.

Swag, DeChambeau and Dick's Sporting Goods are bringing the mystery

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

I'm an unapologetic Swag Golf fan. I love the limited drops of cool headcovers and golf apparel. Honestly, I'm a sucker for anything ultra-limited. I'm also quick to jump on anything cool that comes in a blind box or bag. The surprise sucks me in every time (and I love great golf gifts in general).

So when I found out one of the best golfers in the world, Bryson DeChambeau (he's also arguably the most famous thanks in part to his massive YouTube channel and its Break 50 series), was partnering with Swag to bring limited mystery headcovers to Dick's Sporting Goods and Golf Galaxy, I was excited.

When I got the chance to talk to DeChambeau himself about the headcovers, I had to find out where the inspiration came from. It turns out we really do "have so much time and so little to do."

If you're a fan of Bryson DeChambeau and enjoy a bit of mystery, this is a cool item to snag. Each of the 13 designs features Break 50 logos and fan-chosen designs. Four people also get to meet Bryson, go behind the scenes of a Break 50 episode, hit the range and have lunch with DeChambeau. And even if you don't win, 50 headcovers have his autograph, which gives you something else to chase.

Okay, so officially Willy Wonka has nothing to do with this project, but according to Bryson DeChambeau, "we wanted to give fans an opportunity to experience and feel something different." A blind box isn't new, though. There are lots of products in the golf space (and pop culture in general) that come in blind boxes.

But this one is a little different: "Being a Willy Wonka fan growing up, we thought there could be something cool with a golden ticket. And that golden ticket turned into a golden headcover with four different tiers."

The highest tier isn't just an incredibly limited headcover, though. The people who are lucky enough to find one at Dick's or Golf Galaxy actually get to hang out with Bryson. "They're gonna be going behind the scenes with a Break 50. They're going to have lunch with me, and I'm gonna take them to the driving range. We're gonna have a great day."

Of course, there are other prizes (besides the 13 cool headcovers). There are 50 signed headcovers in the mix, plus there are different tiers of headcovers. Tier 2 features two limited designs that are hard to come by, tier 3 has slightly more common designs, and tier 4 has the most common.

Get instant access to breaking news, the hottest reviews, great deals and helpful tips.

One of the main issues with Swag is that their prices are high, with some headcovers going for more than $100 because they're limited. But this blind bag sells for $59, which is quite reasonable for a high-end headcover. "It's not at a crazy price point, DeChambeau noted. "Eventually, we want to do more stuff at even lower price points," to "grow the game of golf and grow the fan base."

But if you win, don't expect to hit a shot over Bryson's house during filming. "Insurance would probably not be happy about that," Bryson chuckled when I brought up the idea. It looks like his windows will stay safe... for now.

#3046. Alphabet vs. Apple: Which Warren Buffett Stock Is the Better Buy?

Both are major positions in the Berkshire Hathaway portfolio.

By Adria Cimino – Nov 18, 2026 at 6:25PM EST

Warren Buffett is known for selecting quality stocks and holding on to them for many years. And he's proven that this strategy works: For 60 years as chief executive officer at Berkshire Hathaway, Buffett helped deliver market-beating returns. Buffett retired at the beginning of this year, but he remains involved at Berkshire Hathaway and now holds the title of chairman emeritus.

Greg Abel, who took over from Buffett as CEO, has maintained the investing giant's principles and continues to hold many Buffett favorites. Two in particular remain among the company's top holdings: Apple (AAPL +0.15%) and Alphabet (GOOG +0.58%) (GOOGL +0.80%), in the No. 1 and No. 4 positions, respectively.

Which of these Warren Buffett stocks is the better buy today? Let's find out.

Warren Buffett. Image source: Getty Images.

This year marks a 10-year anniversary for Apple and Berkshire Hathaway: Buffett initially bought Apple shares in 2016. And over the years, it's become a cherished position, with Buffett on multiple occasions praising Tim Cook, who held the CEO role until September of this year.

Cook, who recently retired, helped the company's market value soar 1,000% to $4 trillion and revenue almost quadruple to $416 billion in the 2025 fiscal year. Now, with John Ternus at the helm, there's reason to be optimistic about more growth ahead. Ternus, formerly the company's senior vice president of hardware engineering, has the product experience and knowledge to lead Apple through new waves of innovation. Products are Apple's strength, with the iPhone the global smartphone leader, so a product focus is key for future growth.

Apple stock has climbed more than that of fellow tech giants this year, as it isn't as heavily linked to artificial intelligence (AI) -- and therefore is less sensitive to AI-related risks. Though Apple stock isn't cheap, it still could have room to run.

Buffett opened a position in Alphabet last year, and Abel added to it this year, showing clear commitment to this Buffett selection. Alphabet climbed earlier this year but has been under pressure in recent times, along with other tech giants that are investing heavily in AI. Some investors worry that these players are overspending and that the revenue opportunity will be disappointing.

Still, Alphabet's Google Cloud business hasn't faltered; in fact, that unit's revenue has been soaring amid strong demand for its AI products and services. In the recent quarter, Google Cloud revenue jumped 103% to $30 billion. The company has also begun selling its own chips, known as TPUs, to certain customers for use in their data centers. Though this is a small source of revenue right now, it could grow in time.

Meanwhile, Google's search business continues to hold 90% of the search market worldwide, and this has been its ticket to enormous revenue year after year. This is through advertising, with advertisers eager to place ads across the platform to reach potential customers. In the recent quarter, Google ads brought in $82 billion in revenue for the company.

Alphabet stock today looks cheap, particularly compared with that of Apple.

There's a clear reason for this, however, and this goes back to the point I made above: Some investors are turning away from AI big spenders. But this opens up an opportunity to buy Alphabet at a fantastic price.

This depends on your investment style and priorities. From a valuation perspective, Alphabet clearly stands out. Today, this tech giant is a great deal for growth investors looking for a bargain. Though current market sentiment may continue to weigh on Alphabet stock, this doesn't change the company's long-term story.

Alphabet's search business remains solid, and the company's cloud business is well-established and growing -- and doesn't rely uniquely on AI. So investors may benefit if they buy now and hold on as the storm passes.

However, Apple, less linked to AI spending and a long-term Buffett favorite, may be a better buy for the cautious investor. Though the stock has climbed, the release of new products just recently could boost revenue and lead to more gains ahead.

They just revealed what they believe are the 10 best stocks for investors to buy right now, available when you join Stock Advisor.

#3045. Nvidia’s Earnings Calmed Markets, but AI’s Harsh Reckoning Marches On

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

Jensen Huang's departure was only a symptom of a bigger problem in the AI economy.

Though Nvidia’s earnings call Tuesday offered some reassurance to investors after Jensen Huang’s departure as CEO, it did little to resolve the political, regulatory and leadership questions hanging over the entire AI economy.

The semiconductor giant at the heart of the AI infrastructure boom saw its shares plunge by 35% from last month’s high following the scandal around Huang — wiping out nearly $2 trillion in market cap and triggering Wall Street panic around a broader AI reckoning.

Nvidia’s finance chief and interim CEO, Colette Kress, sought to project stability, telling investors it was “business as usual” and raising Nvidia’s fiscal 2028 outlook. Though the chipmaker’s stock price rebounded after reporting better-than-expected results, it’s likely to face a rocky future, as concerns linger around national security violations amid illegal export deals. Market confidence is also shaky amid supply chain backlogs for advanced chips and servers that remain critical to AI compute and the training of large language models.

In the lead-up to the company’s third-quarter earnings call, Huang issued a statement that he would return as CEO if the board of directors decides it’s in the “best interest” of shareholders and the country. Despite calls from President Donald Trump and other tech executives to reinstate the former GPU boss, Harvey C. Jones, Nvidia’s appointed first-ever chair of the board, said there is no intention to reinstate Huang, and no successor was named.

Prior to the call, The Wall Street Journal reported that the US Securities and Exchange Commission is investigating Huang over concerns that the company misled investors.

Huang maneuvered Nvidia into a linchpin role for frontier AI labs and data center firms, not just by supplying hardware but also by providing equity, credit and financial backing and by mobilizing third-party capital to secure investment for the AI buildout of data centers. Nvidia’s circular financing schemes — in which the chipmaker helps fund a hyperscaler like Meta or Microsoft, which then uses the funds to buy its products — were allegedly a source of contention for Kress and other executives.

Critics have warned that this kind of vendor financing inflated the AI bubble by inflating growth projections and buoying the market valuations of chipmakers and hyperscalers.

According to Reuters, Kress was expected to pull back from some of the riskiest AI financial deals, including investments in OpenAI and AI infrastructure companies such as Lambda. Already, Nvidia has significantly scaled back financing for a $250 billion OpenAI data center deal. During Tuesday’s call, Kress failed to announce a sweeping unwind of Huang-era financing arrangements, focusing more on continuity than retreat.

Kress mentioned that chip producer TSMC, a key Nvidia partner, will continue working with Nvidia despite Huang’s departure. The company also said it was assuming no China data-center compute revenue in its next fourth-quarter outlook.

Nvidia supplies the lion’s share of the hardware necessary for data center construction and model training, and the company has long said that the demand for chips exceeds supply. Shortages of RAM and high-bandwidth memory slow down the production of AI servers and raise prices for cloud companies and other buyers. In an attempt to buy its way out of the bottleneck, Nvidia has committed large sums of money to secure future memory supply.

If Nvidia distances itself too much from its GPU commitments, that could be a problem for the entire tech industry. Semiconductor production is an incredibly specialized field dominated by very few companies, and any changes to the supply chain could have ripple effects on global markets and vendors, including in consumer electronics and cloud services.

Huang’s affair with a Chinese executive connected to the Singapore-based Megaspeed — whom US intelligence claimed was a Chinese intelligence asset — complicated an already tight supply chain. Megaspeed was under investigation for allegedly helping Chinese firms obtain high-value Nvidia chips through channels that violated US export controls.

According to the investment research firm Culper, up to one-fifth of Nvidia’s fiscal-year 2026 compute revenues were driven by movement of some of the company’s most advanced chips into China through nonrestricted intermediary countries in a process called diversion. When advanced Nvidia chips or servers are illegally routed to Chinese buyers, those units are no longer available to legal customers in the US and allied markets.

Huang was influential in shaping the Trump administration’s chip export policies, pushing back against export controls that limited Nvidia’s sales to China. But the recent scandal prompted new Commerce Department restrictions on chip shipments through third countries.

While Kress cited improving supply and sustained demand in raising Nvidia’s outlook, stricter export rules and tighter controls could further constrain capacity by creating more obstacles to compliance and delivery.

Nvidia’s better-than-expected earnings may steady investors in the short term, but any company that supplies essential hardware for the AI buildout is at risk if investor confidence in the industry falters. When Nvidia reports strong growth, AI-focused stocks and funds with large technology holdings are likely to go up. When Nvidia’s outlook weakens, investors worry that AI spending is slowing or that companies have overcommitted.

A handful of big tech companies — colloquially known as the Magnificent Seven — make up a big share of major funds and indices. Five of them account for roughly 30% of the S&P 500 index, and AI overexposure in those businesses translates into overexposure in many individual retirement accounts and 401(k)s. Because index funds hold substantial positions in Nvidia and other large tech companies, an AI selloff could affect retirement balances even for people who don’t own Nvidia shares directly.

The Magnificent Seven are heavily affected by AI stock swings. Alphabet’s stock price sank as Nvidia news aftershocks reverberated through the markets, proving that top firms are not unassailable. Other tech companies heavily focused on AI infrastructure, such as Intel and CoreWeave, likewise saw their share prices decline.

Despite growing concerns about AI dangers, Huang was one of AI’s most aggressive promoters, framing the technology as an economic and national security imperative. In early September, the now-disgraced CEO said that artificial general intelligence, or AGI, had arrived, congratulating OpenAI on its GPT-6 Astra model, which was trained on Nvidia’s Grace Blackwell GPUs.

Huang pushed against recent warnings from AI researchers, calling for unrestrained progress on frontier models and repeatedly arguing against new laws and safety regulations.

Even before Huang’s business and personal scandals came to light, AI companies were gearing up for a post-midterm-election PR campaign, as the technology has become increasingly unpopular and the backlash against data centers has grown. Democratic Party politicians pushed a platform to shift the federal government’s lax approach toward the AI industry. Some Democrats are also poised to craft legislation to prevent an AI industry bailout in the event of a bubble pop.

Amid bipartisan calls for Huang to testify before Congress, many politicians are also seeking to investigate high-ranking members of US intelligence and the Commerce Department for allowing export loopholes or for failing to act on knowledge of Nvidia’s China-related deals.

Kress might be able to lead Nvidia with tact and diligence, separating the company from some of its riskiest deals. But the national security implications of her predecessor’s relationships have opened up a can of worms that no CEO can close on their own.

The federal government is ruled by inertia, but right now there’s some investigative momentum aimed directly at the major AI players. That will almost certainly ice out some investor hype. It’s clear the AI industry is facing a reckoning that was not in the cards just months ago.

#3044. Your Wear OS smartwatch just got a new way to save battery life

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

It could make your Pixel Watch or Samsung Galaxy Watch more power efficient.

Some of the most popular Android smartwatches just got a battery-saving update. Google released an updated version of Google Play Services on Tuesday that can help extend the battery life of a Wear OS smartwatch.

The v26.45 update concerns location tracking during outdoor workouts. Wear OS smartwatch users can now use their phone’s Global Navigation Satellite System to track their location during an outdoor workout, which can conserve battery life by outsourcing the smartwatch’s energy to the smartphone when it isn’t in Battery Saver mode.

Also: Pixel Watch 4 vs. Watch 5: Is Google’s new model worth it?

Constantly communicating with satellites during an outdoor workout can be a battery drain, and every minute of battery life counts when you use your smartwatch for communication, workout tracking, and health monitoring. GPS services, a bright, always-on display, and continuous health sensors like SpO2 can squeeze the most juice out of your smartwatch.

Wear OS software is used by many of the most popular smartwatch brands, including Google, Samsung, and OnePlus. Both the Google Pixel Watch and Samsung Galaxy Watch’s largest batteries last approximately 40 hours. That’s nearly two days of use from two top Android smartwatches, but you’d be surprised how quickly it can drain.

Also: Galaxy Watch 9 review: Health data overload, but built for the future

The update improves battery life without users having to take any action or change any settings — the Google Play Services version 26.45 works quietly under the hood.

That’s not the only Google Play Services update that Wear OS smartwatch users can take advantage of. Another recent update lets users view on their phone a larger preview of any watch face they want to install on their smartwatch.

After thousands of readers were hit by last week's Outlook outage, here is how to tell an everyday technical failure from a real cyberattack

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

When 52,846 readers told us in our poll that they were hit by the Outlook outage, it was a reminder of just how disruptive a single breakdown can be. Anytime an essential service stalls like that, speculation about hackers or a coordinated cyberattack quickly spreads.

The truth is far less dramatic. Almost every widespread outage often comes down to ordinary human error or fragile infrastructure rather than malicious hacking.

If your bank app freezes or your flight gets delayed by a system breakdown, here is how to figure out what is actually going on.

Digital services rely on millions of lines of code and overlapping networks, which means things break constantly.

Most major outages are caused by mundane technical failures and human error, a botched software update, a mistyped setting, a severed underground fiber cable, or a simple power failure. Unless concrete evidence of hacking emerges, an accidental technical error is almost always the safest bet.

In fact, according to the Uptime Institute, human error directly or indirectly contributes to between two-thirds and four-fifths of all data center downtime incidents — and roughly 85 percent of those human-error outages stem from staff failing to follow procedures or flaws in the procedures themselves.

When one major service goes down, it often creates a domino effect that makes other apps look broken too.

Get instant access to breaking news, the hottest reviews, great deals and helpful tips.

A payment failure at a single large bank can stall transfers at several others, and a single cloud provider outage can knock dozens of unrelated websites offline at once.

Rather than trusting panicked posts on social media, check an independent outage tracker like Downdetector to see if other users are reporting problems nationwide, or search for the company’s official status page and verified newsroom account.

Major tech platforms, banks, and airlines maintain live dashboards that explain whether their systems are actually offline or just waiting on another partner to come back up.

The timeline of an outage is often one of the biggest clues to its cause.

When an engineer pushes a bad update or a server overloads, companies can usually undo the change, reroute traffic, and restore service within a few minutes or hours — though widespread system crashes can occasionally take longer to untangle.

Deep-seated cyberattacks, such as ransomware breaches, take much longer to resolve because companies have to lock down their networks, investigate the intrusion, kick intruders out, and rebuild damaged systems from scratch, a process that regularly drags on for days or even weeks.

In the first few hours of a major breakdown, nobody, not even the company experiencing the crash, always knows the exact root cause.

When hackers are behind a major disruption, they often claim credit publicly to demand ransom or score political points, or federal cybersecurity officials eventually confirm a breach after investigating.

Until an affected company or government agency shares verified findings, patience is the best defense against bad rumors.

#3040. Blackstone Digital Infrastructure: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

Amazon keeps a 25% stake in the joint venture, a structure that ties the tenant's fortunes to the portfolio's performance.

By The Motley Fool Team – Nov 18, 2026 at 6:26PM EST

10 stocks we like better than Blackstone Digital Infrastructure Trust ›

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 16, 2026, Blackstone Digital Infrastructure (BXDC +0.18%) announced a deal to acquire a 75% interest in a $5 billion portfolio of 13 powered shell data centers (facilities where the landlord owns the building and power connection while the tenant supplies equipment) leased to Amazon. The deal would transition the trust from holding cash to generating steady rental income backed by an investment-grade technology titan. For long-term investors, this initial purchase establishes the template for how the company plans to scale its portfolio. Three things management said stand out.

Management committed its initial capital to acquire a 75% majority interest in a $5 billion data center portfolio. Amazon retained the remaining 25% minority stake as a joint venture (a cooperative business partnership) under a triple net lease (an agreement where the tenant pays property taxes, insurance, and maintenance costs), ensuring the technology titan remains aligned with the property's performance.

Powered shells have fewer ongoing landlord responsibilities than turnkey facilities. The transaction delivers a 7% going-in capitalization rate (cap rate, the initial annual rental yield on a property investment), along with 2.75% annual rent increases that protect against inflation.

Hyperscalers (large-scale cloud providers requiring vast computing capacity) face massive infrastructure spending demands to support artificial intelligence. Purchasing existing assets through sale-leaseback transactions (where an owner sells property and immediately leases it back) doubles the company's addressable market (the total market opportunity) to over $2 trillion by 2030.

Data center demand is expanding into power-rich regions such as Indiana and Mississippi, where early positioning offers a distinct advantage. Blackstone's platform scale and sourcing pipeline give the trust access to a wide range of on- and off-market deals.

Management is evaluating an expanding pipeline of upcoming asset deliveries while focusing on disciplined capital allocation and tenant alignment.

#3038. Prediction: This Will Be Sandisk’s Stock Price by the End of 2027

Sandisk stock's recent slide isn't justified, and I won't be surprised to see a big rally over the coming year.

By Harsh Chauhan – Nov 18, 2026 at 6:30PM EST

After an incredible rally in the first half of 2026, shares of Sandisk (SNDK -2.64%) have pulled back at an alarming pace in recent months.

The stock has retreated 60% since the beginning of July. Investors have been booking profits in Sandisk stock for various reasons. For instance, the broader semiconductor industry has been under pressure in recent months, as Wall Street is concerned about whether the incredible pace of spending on artificial intelligence (AI) infrastructure is sustainable amid rising inflation, which has led the Federal Reserve to increase interest rates.

However, this isn't the only reason why Sandisk stock has taken a beating. Concerns about the state of the NAND flash industry that Sandisk serves have played a central role in the stock's decline. Let's look at the factors impacting Sandisk stock lately and check if it can step on the gas in 2027.

Sandisk manufactures NAND flash storage products, including enterprise solid-state drives (SSDs) that are used in AI data centers. The shortage of traditional hard-disk drives (HDDs) forced hyperscalers and AI companies to turn to SSDs to store huge data sets for training and inference workloads.

The demand for NAND flash storage significantly outpaced supply, creating a major shortage that led to a sharp price spike. Market research firm Gartner estimates that NAND flash prices will rise by 293% in 2026, as demand exceeds supply by 9%. The shortage is expected to persist into 2027, with demand exceeding supply by 5%. As a result, Gartner predicts a 30% increase in NAND flash prices next year.

This slower increase in prices has spooked investors, who now believe that Sandisk's days of hypergrowth could be coming to an end. Meanwhile, TrendForce reports that weak consumer demand for NAND has led to a 20% drop in spot prices in just two weeks. The shortage of data center graphics cards in China has led to a decline in enterprise SSD demand, thereby lowering prices.

These divergent views about the state of the NAND flash market in 2027 have weighed on Sandisk's shares recently. However, it is worth noting that lower spot prices don't necessarily mean that the NAND flash market will enter a downward spiral next year. That's because spot contracts usually involve small lot sizes and reportedly account for only 5% of NAND flash revenue.

Contract prices decide the market's direction, as NAND flash customers like to plan well in advance for their needs. This explains why Sandisk has already sold a large chunk of its NAND flash supply for the next two fiscal years under long-term agreements.

Sandisk management noted on the October earnings call that it has already sold 50% of its supply capacity for fiscal 2027 under long-term contracts. Even better, the company has demand commitments for two-thirds of its fiscal 2028 capacity. Management added that its long-term contract pipeline exceeds its supply capacity through the end of the decade.

So, Sandisk has demand visibility for the long run, which explains why its financial performance in fiscal 2027, which has just begun, is going to be solid.

Sandisk's revenue in the first quarter of fiscal 2027 (which ended Oct. 2) increased by 378% year over year to $11 billion. The company's non-GAAP earnings per share jumped from just $1.22 in the year-ago period to $49.51 in the previous quarter.

Importantly, analysts have become bullish on Sandisk's earnings growth prospects for the next three years.

SNDK EPS Estimates for Current Fiscal Year data by YCharts

Additionally, the recent pullback in this semiconductor stock has brought its price-to-earnings (P/E) ratio to just 7. The forward earnings multiple is even more attractive at 3.5. Sandisk is a screaming buy at these multiples, considering the impressive earnings growth it is poised to clock.

Assuming Sandisk's earnings jump to $289.05 in fiscal 2028 (which will end in June 2028) and it continues to trade at 7 times earnings, its stock price could reach $2,023 in a year and a half. That implies potential gains of 145%. Meanwhile, the stock has a 12-month median price target of $2,100, suggesting that Wall Street is even more bullish about its prospects over the coming year.

So, Sandisk can deliver significant upside by the end of 2027, which is why it would make sense to buy this AI stock following its recent decline before it steps on the gas once again.

#3037. Fathom Forthcoming: First Battery Cells Roll Out of Ford’s Michigan Plant for Electric Pickup

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

Ford's BlueOval Battery Park Michigan is shipping its first lithium iron phosphate cells to Louisville for Fathom prototypes, a key milestone for the automaker's next-generation EV.

This week, Ford began shipments of the first lithium iron phosphate electric vehicle battery cells from its new BlueOval Battery Park in Marshall, Michigan, to its Louisville Assembly Plant in Kentucky. There, the prismatic cells will power early Ford Fathom electric pickup truck prototypes and validation vehicles. The automaker invited me into the plant to meet the people who build the batteries and to see how the so-called “jelly roll” is made.

In 2025, consumers lost access to the clean vehicle tax rebates of the Inflation Reduction Act, but the program and its incentives remain in the law for automakers and suppliers. So bringing this US-based, domestically owned battery plant online is kind of a big deal for Ford. It’s an important milestone for the automaker’s plan to make its new Universal Electric Vehicle, or UEV, platform a reality and to bring the Fathom — its upcoming electric pickup truck — to market in 2027 at a price that EV-eager customers can stomach.

Ford expects to sell the upcoming Fathom at an estimated price of $28,350 (well, $29,945, including the $1,595 destination and delivery fee), with the first preorder expected to open early next year.

BlueOval Battery Park Michigan is a $3 billion, 2 million-square-foot facility with a targeted annual production capacity of 20 gigawatt-hours. That’s a whole lot of batteries. Ford is expected to employ 1,700 people at the site across two 12-hour shifts, running around the clock to reach that capacity.

The plant is a clean facility, so getting in meant donning a clean suit, mask, hairnet, gloves and two layers of shoe booties. I then crossed special magnetic and sticky floor panels designed to pull any metal particles off the bottoms of those booties, before passing through multiple air-blower airlocks that blasted my body clean of loose dust.

From the outside, the building is huge and difficult to take in completely. On the inside, the scale truly comes into focus as I stare down a massive hallway just under a mile long end to end. Raw materials — rolls of copper and aluminum sheet, plus the chemical powders that coat them — enter the plant at the eastern end, move in a straight line through the facility, and completed battery packs exit at the western end. The first 0.7 mile of the fab belongs to cell production, split between isolated cathode and anode lines that join halfway through for final cell assembly.

Ford engineers, battery experts and executives tell me that the automaker chose lithium iron phosphate, or LFP, chemistry in a prismatic (rectangular) format for its affordability and structural benefits. LFP uses no nickel or cobalt, which keeps costs down, and Ford found the chemistry well suited to bidirectional charging.

To keep costs low enough to deliver a reasonably priced car, Ford couldn’t use a Chinese supplier and still qualify for IRA manufacturing credits. So it licensed the technology from one, working with a company called CATL, which one Ford exec described as “the world’s leading battery manufacturer,” whose batteries power 30 million vehicles worldwide.

Despite working with a Chinese company, Ford says the plant is completely US-owned, staffed and operated and is totally IRA-compliant, with no foreign entity of concern, or FEOC, involvement beyond the licensing agreement.

Charles Poon, who leads vehicle hardware engineering, said Ford didn’t simply copy a catalog part.

“We designed a unique cell that does not exist in the CATL catalog, specifically for the UEV,” Poon said, adding that the taller and simpler cell delivers “the energy density, the affordability, the durability that [Ford is] looking for” and allows the automaker to more completely integrate the cells and battery pack into the vehicle’s design.

Ford also had to build the supply chain, Poon said, sourcing an IRA-compliant stream for a battery chemistry that is only just entering the mainstream in North America, and working with approved global partners and North American suppliers.

On the cathode and anode assembly lines, meter-wide rolls of copper and aluminum are first coated with a chemical slurry that is baked on. The large rolls are then split into 10 smaller rolls, with tabs notched into their exposed edges at set intervals. As the continuous rolls pass through multiple machines like gigantic cassette players, AI-powered vision systems watch the foil for coating defects, scratches and wrinkles, marking problem sections so they can be automatically removed.

Next, the cathode and anode foils are layered and rolled into a cylinder, then pressed flat into what Ford calls a jelly roll. Imagine a roll of toilet paper mashed in a hydraulic press, and you’ll have a good idea of what it looks like: a flat rectangle with rounded ends.

The jelly rolls are then gathered in pairs, and the previously exposed tabs are folded together and ultrasonically welded to more solid terminals, which are in turn laser-welded to a cap. The jelly roll couple is then laser-sealed into an aluminum box, about the size and weight of a masonry brick, to form a nearly complete prismatic cell. Engineers vacuum out the air, check for leaks by pressurizing the cell with helium, and fill it with liquid electrolyte. Bob’s your uncle: you have a finished LFP prismatic cell.

Ford says the assembly lines can deliver 40 cells every minute, which plant manager Tracy Church says makes BlueOval Battery Park “among the fastest LFP prismatic lines outside China.”

From the cell line, completed prismatic cells would normally pass through a door and straight into pack manufacturing, where full battery packs with integrated “e-box” electronics, integrated cooling and wiring are built. That part of the plant is still undergoing validation, so for now Ford is shipping just the cells to Louisville, not complete battery packs. The company plans to have pack manufacturing up and running in Marshall ahead of Fathom’s launch, with full pack deliveries to Louisville planned in 2027. Church told me that the plant aims to produce 50 battery packs per hour.

Ford hasn’t officially specified the energy capacity of the cells or the completed battery packs and wouldn’t comment when asked about expected range for the Fathom. The automaker wouldn’t even confirm how many cells go into the EV’s pack, but I estimate between 90 and 108 cells based on a cutaway of the finalized unit I examined (but wasn’t permitted to photograph) during my tour. I expect Ford is holding off on finalizing details until closer to the EV’s 2027 launch window.

Battery Park Michigan isn’t just sending cells to Kentucky; it’s also sending expertise. Ford’s Glendale Kentucky plant — which used to build batteries for the F-150 Lightning full-size electric pickup — is currently being retooled to build LFP cells for Ford Energy for large-scale energy storage applications, and the lessons Ford learns in Michigan are already being shared with the manufacturing teams there.

Ford has been in the EV business for over 15 years. In 2011, it revealed the Focus Electric at CES. 2019 brought the debut of the Mustang Mach-E — which Ford recently found is holding up well after years on the road with minimal battery degradation — followed by the arrival of the F-150 Lightning in 2021. This isn’t the brand’s first electric rodeo, but executives tell me that the automaker has never had this much control and ownership over its battery production, and it’s hoping that will make the difference as it prepares the next-gen UEV vehicles.

During Gewirtz’s test, when the agent was part of his $20 per month Google AI Pro plan, he used only 20% of the total five hours of usage on “four fairly substantial tests.” Without an AI plan, Gemini’s context window is 32k tokens.

At launch in September, Meta CEO Mark Zuckerberg said Muse is “free to use for up to 100M tokens per week,” though Meta’s help center is not so explicit. As with most agentic products, more frequent users may want to buy more access, but more casual users now have two options to compare.

Also: Don’t use Muse? How Meta’s AI agent can still access your private messages

As with other agents, Tasks, which runs on its newest Gemini 4 Hydrogen model, can handle a variety of jobs for you, from grocery shopping to booking appointments and analyzing your heating bill. Agents like these are designed for a chronically overwhelmed society trained by a reliance on tech to seek convenience and productivity in nearly every aspect of life.

The feature is free in the US, Canada, Korea, Japan, Australia, and India. US users can also start tasks by emailing or texting Gemini, and can have the agent call businesses for them (except in Indiana, Louisiana, Minnesota, Montana, and Nebraska).

The change from Spark to Tasks comes just a day after Google retired Gems (which let you tailor Gemini to specific tasks), and converted existing Gems into Skills. The new landing page for Tasks emphasizes personal finance and cost-cutting as a use case, positioning itself alongside personal finance features from Anthropic and OpenAI.

“Put Gemini on the hunt for ways to save. Identify recurring expenses you no longer need, get alerts for your most-desired deals, and more,” the page reads.

Also: Want to try ChatGPT Wallet? Read OpenAI’s liability disclaimer

“You can adjust task settings, disconnect Google and third-party apps, or delete chat history anytime,’ Google’s blog on the release said. Google’s new Memory summary feature for Gemini lets users view and edit the personal info the chatbot has on them.

#3035. Is Alphabet Stock a Buy Right Now?

The stock's upside far outweighs its potential downside.

By Stefon Walters – Nov 18, 2026 at 6:37PM EST

It has been a rough year for the "Magnificent Seven" stocks as a whole, with four in the negative for the year through market close on Nov. 13. Among the four is Google's parent company Alphabet (GOOG +0.58%)(GOOGL +0.80%), which is down 5.5%, including a 12.6% single-day drop on Oct. 29 that saw the company lose more value in one day than ever before in its history.

With Alphabet in the red and underperforming (the S&P 500 is up 10.5%), is now a good time to invest in the stock, or should investors take it as a warning sign and steer clear of a falling knife?

Alphabet's stock struggles this year are more about how much it's spending than how much it's making. In the third quarter, Alphabet made $127.6 billion in revenue, up 25% year over year, and its operating income (profit from its core business) increased nearly 30% to $40.6 billion.

Unfortunately, Alphabet's free cash flow was negative, with a $9.1 billion deficit, because of high capital expenditures (capex). It spent $51.4 billion in the quarter, most of it on AI-related projects (data centers, custom chips, and other infrastructure).

High spending has been the recent norm for hyperscalers like Alphabet, Amazon (AMZN +3.82%), Microsoft (MSFT +0.41%), and Meta Platforms (META -0.40%), but it's easy to see why investors value free cash flow. That's money used for things like dividends, share buybacks, and other investments.

The spending isn't expected to slow down anytime soon, either. Alphabet projects total 2026 capex of between $195 billion and $205 billion, more than doubling what it spent last year.

GOOGL Capital Expenditures (Annual) data by YCharts

Alphabet has a trifecta working in its favor. First, it has a thriving Google Cloud business that increased revenue 103% to $30.7 billion in Q3. Google Cloud still trails Amazon Web Services and Microsoft Azure in market share (and likely will for the foreseeable future), but it's growing much faster than both.

Cloud progress is one of the clearest indicators of whether a company is seeing a return on its AI investments. Although we're still a ways away from any hyperscaler seeing a clear return on investment, triple-digit growth is encouraging.

The second thing working in Alphabet's favor is a virtual monopoly in Google Search, with a 91% market share. Google Search revenue has slowed over the past couple of quarters, but it still generated $63.4 billion of revenue (up 12%), with elite margins. Alphabet is still working through balancing traditional search and AI convenience, but I don't see that as a long-term issue.

The third thing working in Alphabet's favor -- and why it's especially looking like a buy right now -- is how cheap the stock is at the moment, trading at 15.4 times its earnings. It's not only the cheapest of the "Magnificent Seven" stocks, but also well below the 23.5 it has averaged over the past five years.

At its current valuation, Alphabet has far more upside than downside for long-term investors.

#3034. Not Safe, Not Creative: 123456 Is Still the World’s Most Common Password

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

Tech > Services & Software > Cybersecurity

Data breaches, hacks and cyberattacks. Guess what? Our passwords are still risky.

Our data keeps getting hacked, breached, stolen and exposed, and yet we’re still using the most predictable passwords.

For the seventh time in the past eight years, 123456 is the world’s most common password, and it’s got other uncreative companions on the list, according to the latest analysis by password and passkey manager NordPass, a division of Nord Security. The top three most used passwords in the US were: 123456, password and admin. Yikes.

In the 44 countries analyzed, the password 123456 was used more than 7.2 million times. The rest of the top 10 passwords: admin, 12345678, 123456789, 1234, 12345, password, Aa123456, Pass@123 and 1234567890.

NordPass said that even though many people prefer simple, easy-to-remember passwords, much more awareness of password safety is needed. The company recommends creating a unique password for each account that’s at least 15 characters long, including uppercase and lowercase letters, numbers and special characters. Though it’s tempting to reuse the same passwords, hackers rely on that complacency.

NordPass suggests upgrading weak, old passwords to stronger ones. You can also add multifactor authentication, which requires an additional layer of security beyond a login password. That could mean entering a one-time passcode sent by text or email, answering security questions, or using facial recognition or fingerprint ID.

CNET managing editor Adam Benjamin said that the risk isn’t primarily from someone randomly guessing your password. The danger is from algorithms that collect data or bots that use credential stuffing (in other words, stolen username and password pairs obtained from a data breach).

According to Benjamin, using an obscure reference or a different language doesn’t make a password stronger. “A password is good when it’s too much trouble for an algorithm to crack,” he said.

You might remember the scene from the 1987 movie Spaceballs, where the combination to Planet Druidia’s air shield is 12345. Dark Helmet calls it “the stupidest combination I’ve ever heard in my life!” and says it’s “the kind of thing an idiot would have on his luggage.” The joke remains a staple reference for weak passwords and PINs, and yet, here we are.

Last year, two researchers hacked into McDonald’s hiring chatbot by guessing the password 123456 to access 64 million data records, including applicant names, email addresses and phone numbers.

In 2023, the Akira ransomware group hacked into KNP Logistics Group, a 158‑year‑old UK transportation firm, by guessing an employee’s weak password. The hackers locked the company’s data and demanded a $6.6 million ransom, forcing KNP to shut down and lay off 700 workers.

This year, suspected Iranian hackers gained access to US water supply systems due to internet-connected devices that used default passwords — or none at all.

The rise of the AI era has also made password hacking easier. One study even found that some AI systems could determine people’s passwords by listening to their keystrokes.

NordPass has conducted its Top 200 Most Common Passwords analysis since 2019. To compile its lists, NordPass and independent researchers examined passwords exposed in public data breaches and dark web repositories, then organized and categorized the data by country of origin.

NordPass has found that password habits are heavily influenced by current trends and culture. Over the last several years, some of the top three passwords have been Lebron0319 (in reference to NBA star LeBron James), Savage20 (musical group) and roblox.com1 (video game). In the 2010s, the top three were Lachimolala (meme), telegram (social media) and minecraft (video game).

But while these passwords might make us feel nostalgic for these time capsules of experiences, interests and entertainment, they don’t translate to strong internet security. “Unfortunately, fandoms make for terrible passwords,” the NordPass report notes.

Passwords based on pop culture, current events and sports can be easily predicted or cracked in seconds using automated tools, especially if your interests are visible on public social media profiles. Adding a number to the end of a password doesn’t help — attackers program their tools to test common number combinations and symbol swaps.

Given all the accounts we deal with — financial institutions, social media, stores, government agencies — creating dozens of unique passwords and remembering them can be exhausting and annoying.

“Inventing passwords that meet that criterion across hundreds of logins is virtually impossible, which is why we recommend password managers to create long, complex passwords and then store them all for you,” said Benjamin.

A password manager creates and updates passwords for apps, Wi-Fi networks and websites, ensuring that all your passwords are unique and resistant to bot attacks. Another plus is that you don’t have to remember them or write them down: They’re stored in an encrypted vault you can access by verifying your identity. Password managers can also auto-fill login credentials.

CNET senior writer Joe Supan said the best password managers also offer passkeys, which are ways of logging into apps and websites using biometric ID, such as facial or fingerprint scans, or PIN codes. “These are really the sign-in methods of the future, and I probably wouldn’t recommend a password manager that doesn’t include them at this point,” Supan said.

CNET’s top two recommendations for password managers are NordPass and Bitwarden.

#3033. 5 new Apple Health features you should try in iOS 27.3 – if your phone qualifies

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Apple's redesigned Health app arrives in December with some major upgrades, but not every iPhone will get the full experience.

Apple is kicking off a new and improved Health app next month with the release of iOS 27.3. Instead of just recording and displaying your health-related stats, the app will tap into Apple Intelligence to analyze the data and dispense advice on how to improve your health and fitness. But that reliance on AI means you’ll need the right type of iPhone to enjoy all the benefits.

Also: Apple could build its AI health chatbot from this study – how to enroll

Here are five helpful new features built into the app, along with what you’ll need to access them.

First up, the app will offer suggestions based on data from different sources. For example, it might advise you to go for a walk at a nearby park if it detects that you’re not getting enough exercise. It may show you articles and videos about sleep if it finds that you’re not well-rested. It could also highlight possible conditions based on your lab and test results. You can easily view these recommendations from the Insights screen.

Also: Health is Tim Cook’s defining legacy – and your Apple Watch proves it

A new feature called Health Age compares your physical health to your actual age to show how you’re doing. The analysis draws on certain stats and lab data, such as your resting heart rate, sleep, heart rate variability (HRV), cholesterol, and A1C (average blood glucose levels), to deliver a health age, which may differ from your chronological age. The results can show you areas that may be of concern or that you need to focus on.

Also: Apple Watch Series 12 review: Finally, the health tracker I’ve been waiting for

With a new feature called Movement Evaluations and an iPhone and Apple Watch, the Health app can assess your flexibility, strength, balance, VO2 max (maximum oxygen absorption), and overall movement. You perform a physical routine while your iPhone “watches” you via the camera. Using built-in AI, the app will provide audio and visual feedback in real-time to tell you how you’re faring.

Apple promises that the analysis is performed on your device without recording, storing, or sharing the video.

The new app will be able to correlate your test results with data collected by your iPhone and Apple Watch to give you a more comprehensive picture of your health. Plus, Apple and Quest Diagnostics have teamed up to offer people in the US an optional $119 bloodwork panel that covers more than 50 biomarkers to scan for different health and medical conditions.

Also: I tested the Apple Watch Series 12 heart-rate claims against my gold-standard Polar strap

Normally, you need an Apple Fitness+ subscription to fire up workout videos to help you exercise. But the new Health app will come with its own comprehensive set of training videos. Available from the Insights section, a series of strength exercise videos are designed to help you build your body strength. The videos cover all the key areas of your body to give you a full workout.

Some of the new features, such as the strength videos, will be available to all iPhone users. But the ones that rely on AI will run only on phones that support Apple Intelligence. That means you’ll need an iPhone 15 Pro or Pro Max, any iPhone 16 or 17, an iPhone 18 Pro or Pro Max, an iPhone Air, or an iPhone Duo.

#3031. 5 Student Loan Scams to Avoid in 2026

We believe everyone should be able to make financial decisions with confidence. While we don't cover every company or financial product on the market, we work hard to share a wide range of offers and objective editorial perspectives.

So how do we make money? Our partners compensate us for advertisements that appear on our site. This compensation helps us provide tools and services - like free credit score access and monitoring. With the exception of mortgage, home equity and other home-lending products or services, partner compensation is one of several factors that may affect which products we highlight and where they appear on our site. Other factors include your credit profile, product availability and proprietary website methodologies.

However, these factors do not influence our editors' opinions or ratings, which are based on independent research and analysis. Our partners cannot pay us to guarantee favorable reviews. Here is a list of our partners.

Student loan scams use pressure tactics and make false promises. Here’s what to watch out for.

Student loan scams can be costly, but they usually leave clues. Scammers will often try to hook victims with tempting claims of “act now or miss out” and “we promise to get you student aid."

The communication might seem realistic, but unexpected fees, unknown contacts and big promises are usually red flags. The age-old adage is true: if an offer sounds too good to be true, it probably is.

Here’s a breakdown of common student loan scams and what to do about them.

» MORE: How to spot student loan repayment scams

The scam: A fraudulent private company offers to manage your student loans and help you get them relieved or even fully forgiven. These services can cost more than $1,000, and there’s no guarantee that you’ll actually get the help you’re looking for.

How to avoid it: You can manage your federal student loans for free. According to the U.S. Department of Education, you never have to pay for help with your federal student loans[1]U.S. Department of Education. Avoiding Student Aid Scams. View all sources . You can work with your loan servicer to adjust your payment plan, consolidate loans and apply for loan forgiveness at no cost to you.

If you’re being promised immediate student loan forgiveness, are asked for personal identification information like your FAFSA login credentials or asked to authorize a power of attorney, proceed with caution — it’s likely a scam.

» MORE: How to get help with student loans

The scam: A company charges you an upfront fee to combine multiple student loans into one new loan to reduce your monthly payment. The company may be illegitimate and is likely to pocket the fee without actually consolidating your loans.

How to avoid it: You can consolidate federal student loans for free. There is no fee to consolidate your loans through the Department of Education at studentaid.gov. If you’re looking to reduce your monthly payment through consolidation, start by contacting your loan servicer. And if you get unsolicited communication from a company promising federal student loan consolidation, it's probably a scam.

The scam: Scammers use transitions in federal loan programs to take advantage of borrowers. For example, the Education Department was set to resume wage garnishments after a long pause, but in January 2026 announced a temporary delay implement repayment reforms.

When federal decisions go back and forth like this, it creates a gray area for scammers to make wage garnishment threats. For borrowers who may not know if wage garnishment restarted, it can be hard to know what to do. This goes for other federal changes, too; any uncertainty can be an opportunity for bad actors to take advantage of the confusion.

How to avoid it: If your wages are truly being garnished, the communication will come from the federal government and not a private company. Likewise, if a company offers to stop your wage garnishment for a fee, you’re probably getting scammed.

The scam: Criminals impersonate a real person and apply to colleges in their name. Then, they can collect financial aid, take out loans and use their college admission to net discounts on goods and services. This is known as a “ghost student” scam.

How to avoid it: Follow the steps on identitytheft.gov to report fraud and get a recovery plan. There are a few safeguards you can put in place to prevent this form of identity theft, such as setting up a credit freeze and monitoring your FSA ID activity.

How it works: You might receive a call from someone claiming to be from your school’s financial aid office saying you have unpaid tuition. The caller may threaten that you cannot start your classes until your tuition bill is paid in full. Scammers can be creative and convincing, even going so far as to replicate a school’s phone number to display on your caller ID.

How to avoid it: The Federal Trade Commission warns that schools will not call and pressure you to pay tuition[2]Federal Trade Commission. Got a call about unpaid college tuition? It could be a scam. Accessed Nov 16, 2026. View all sources . Rather than trust the caller, use your school's secure student portal to check your account balance. Know for certain what you owe and to whom before sending money. You can also call your financial aid office to confirm the legitimacy of a phone number that has called you requesting tuition funds.

Realizing you’ve been scammed is stressful, and it’s natural to feel overwhelmed or embarrassed. But acting quickly can make a real difference. The sooner you report what happened, the better your chances of limiting the financial damage and getting your accounts back on track. Here’s what to do:

If you’ve already paid a scammer, report the problem to the bank that issued the payment. If you paid with a credit card, for instance, contact the company and tell them what happened. They may be able to freeze your account or reverse the transaction.

Reach out to your federal student loan servicer as well and report what happened. They can check to make sure no changes have been made to your account and, in the case that changes did occur, they can help you get your loan back on track.

File complaints with the Education Department, the Federal Trade Commission and the Consumer Financial Protection Bureau. Filing complaints with these agencies helps hold scammers accountable, can result in enforcement action or business shutdowns, and in some cases may help you recover lost funds.

#3029. Did Christopher Nolan Just Bring 4K Blu-Ray Discs Back to Stores?

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

The Odyssey hit the shelves of Best Buy, just in time for the holidays.

Physical media lovers, take note: Best Buy has begun selling 4K Ultra HD Blu-ray discs in-store and online. If you’ve been paying attention, you’ll know how big a pivot this is for the electronics retailer. Back in 2023, the company announced it would cease all sales of DVDs and Blu-rays entirely.

A Best Buy spokesperson didn’t immediately reply to our request for comment.

When the retailer first announced it would be ditching DVD and Blu-ray sales, it cited the decades-long decline in physical media sales — which fell from its peak revenue benchmark of $16 billion in 2005 to less than $1 billion in 2024 — and planned to populate the store space with other tech-based products.

The Odyssey, the nearly 3-hour-long big-screen adaptation of Homer’s epic poem, follows King Odysseus (played by Matt Damon) as he leads his soldiers on a 10-year journey home after the Trojan War. It features a stacked slate of acting talent from Anne Hathaway and Tom Holland to Zendaya, Jon Bernthal and Charlize Theron, and earned $1.8 billion at the global box office.

Those details, along with the timing of the coming holiday shopping season, have apparently inspired Best Buy to change course.

It should be noted that physical media still has value for many people. In an era when movies and TV shows (purchased or otherwise) are suddenly pulled from streamers, the allure of owning titles on DVD and Blu-ray endures. Not to mention, 4K Blu-rays offer better picture quality than their compressed, streaming versions.
